Weinberg’s move on the cosmological constant is the rare case where the feeling was turned into a number. If Λ is drawn from a wide range, the observers who measure it should find it small enough for galaxies, and not much smaller than that unless something else is at work. The measured value is near that edge. That is interesting. It is not a proof of a menu. It is what a filter looks like when you bother to write it down.

The year was 1987. Galaxies, in the working picture, grow from the twitches of Chapter 14 only if the shove does not win too early. If vacuum energy is large, the stretch accelerates before the overdensities can collapse, and you get a thin fog with no spirals and no kitchens. Weinberg asked what values of Λ a later observer could possibly report. The answer was an upper bound: not much larger than the matter density at the epoch when galaxies form. If the prior is wide — if many values are on offer, and nothing else picks — then typical observers should find themselves near that edge, not deep in a valley of needless smallness. Eleven years later the bombs of Chapter 18 found a shove of that order. That is the interesting part. It is not a photograph of other valleys. It is a number that *looks like* a cut. Hot as a calculation: if you grant the ensemble, the bound is a bound. Warm as a hint: the measured Λ sits where a filter would put it. Cold as a proof that the ensemble exists.

The tidal seas belong here as a humility. We thought “arranged for life” meant a Goldilocks band and a blue marble. Then we found that most of the wet real estate in the solar system might be dark, under ice, squeezed by a giant, uninterested in sunsets. If there is life there, it did not need our sermon. If there is not, the permit was still wider than the sermon. Arrangement, if you must use the word, is sloppy and local, not a tailored suit.

Chapter 28 drew the starlight band. Chapter 29 drew the squeeze. The sermon had been: liquid water under a yellow sun, a blue marble, a sky, a kitchen window. The inventory, once you stop flattering the window, is ruder. Europa’s ocean, if you could unroll it, would shame the Pacific. Enceladus vents a kitchen’s ingredients into space. Ganymede may stack seas between ices. A rogue world, thrown into the dark, can keep a sea under a thermos of ice without ever having had a sunset worth naming. None of that is a detection of life. All of it is a detection of *permit*. The permit is wider than the postcard. If you were going to crown a world for being arranged, you would have to crown the dark ones too, and they do not care about your crown. Humility, here, is not a mood. It is a map correction. Life, if it is a water trick, was never a blue-marble exclusive. Observers who need a sky to grow basil are a narrower guild. Do not confuse the guild with the cosmos.

The Weak Anthropic Principle, in a careful wording, is a selection effect: the constants we measure are drawn from the values that permit us to measure them. Carbon chemists need carbon chemistry. They need a universe old enough to have cooked carbon in stars. They need a shove that did not win too early. Saying so is as taut as saying that fish find themselves in water. It is also as useful as that, once you have an ensemble to select from.

Barrow and Tipler, who wrote the big book that put the names in order, said the weak principle this way: the observed values of the constants are not equally probable. They are restricted by the requirement that there be sites where carbon-based life can evolve, and by the requirement that the universe be old enough that it already has. That is a likelihood cut. It is not a cause. It does not cook the carbon. It does not aim the shove. It tells you which draws from a menu you are allowed to be surprised by. Carter’s original wording was in that neighborhood. The later sermons wandered off.

The kitchen version is a fish. A fish, if fish wrote books, would write a hymn to water. The hymn would be true as a feeling: water is the condition of the gill. The hymn would be a leap as a cosmology: water was arranged for fish. The honest sentence is that you do not find fish composing treatises in a dry valley. A child at the sink, watching the tap, is not puzzled that cooking happens in a wet room. The child would be puzzled to find a casserole on Venus. The weak principle is that lack of puzzlement, written for constants. It stops you from treating your own lungs as a theorem about the loaf.

Without an ensemble, the weak principle is a reminder, not an explanation. It stops you from being surprised that the kitchen is not on Venus. It does not tell you why there is a kitchen.

A reminder is still a tool. It keeps you from writing “therefore God” in the margin of *α*. It keeps you from writing “therefore us” in the margin of Λ. It does not replace a theory of why the numbers have the values they have. If there is only one universe, and it had to have some numbers, then “these numbers permit us” is a consistency check. You already knew you were here. Consistency checks are not nothing. They are not a menu.

With an ensemble — cheap copies, expensive bubbles, a menu of Λ — the weak principle becomes a cut. You throw away the valleys that have no observers, and you look at what is left. You still need a measure. You still need the ensemble to be more than a catalog from an untestable framework. You do not get to say “the universe wanted us.” You get to say “we could not have found ourselves in the valleys that did not want anyone.”

Chapter 39 already sat with the expensive copies. Eternal inflation plus a landscape is the ensemble people reach for. **String theory cannot currently be tested.** A reported 10⁵⁰⁰ vacua, offered without a risky prediction that would kill the framework, is a catalog, not a census. Other frameworks offer other menus. Some offer one valley. The leftover glow does not yet choose. The measure problem sits on any infinite menu like a cat: too many observers, no agreed count, “typical” a fight. Until the fight ends, “the landscape predicted Λ” is a hope with a number attached. It is a better hope than a required last mind, because it does not contradict the shove. It is still a hope. Warm as a research program. Cold as a result.

The Strong Anthropic Principle says the universe *must* permit life. “Must” is a smuggler. It can mean the tautology, the ensemble, or a purpose. Only the first is safe. The Final Anthropic Principle says intelligent processing must arise and must never die out. That is the Omega Point’s moral clause. Chapter 19 already broke its physics. Its morals were never physics.

Unpack the smuggler. “Must,” in the tautology, means: a universe we are looking at, from inside, is one that permitted looking. Allowed. Empty of news. “Must,” in the ensemble, means: among the valleys, the occupied ones are the ones that permit occupation. That is the weak principle again, wearing a louder coat. “Must,” as purpose, means: the loaf was aimed. Aimed by whom, through which channel nature uses, with which leftover in the glow as a serial number? Chapter 34 already asked the watchmaker to clock in. The channel has not spoken. The Final principle is louder still. It does not only want a permit. It wants a forever-mind as a law. The bombs, the leftover glow, and the mixmaster of Chapter 16 declined. Barrow, who had helped write the names, had the better habit in the short origin book this one uses: walk up to the speculation and call it an idea for ideas. The Final Anthropic Principle is an idea for ideas that put on a lab coat. This book will not help it with the buttons.

A filter is not a purpose. A purpose has an aim. A filter has a mesh. We are what got through, in this valley, on this worldline, with this leftover helium. That is enough to be getting on with. It is not enough to crown us.

Do not crown the fish for discovering water. Do not crown Mara for finding a permit that includes basil. Do not crown this species for occupying a temperate crust while dark seas, if they are empty, were always empty without our permission, and if they are not, were never waiting for a sermon. The leftover glow does not have a favorite. The shove does not have a favorite. The mesh does not owe the things that pass through it a parade. You may still grow the basil. You may still melt the ice. You may still be glad the nuclear force was not a few percent ruder. Glad is a local weather. It is not a cosmology.

---

\newpage

## 42. No Cosmic Craftsman

Darwin’s trick, which this book has been circling since the prologue, is that a filter can look like a craftsman if you only see the survivors. The beaks fit the seeds. The fit looks like intent. The intent was death, repeated, in a lot of directions.

The look is not a small look. A living thing is an invoice so long, and so locally paid, that a reasonable person staring at one bird will reach for a someone. Paley’s watch on the heath was that reach, and it was not stupid as a first move. A watch has parts that mesh. A finch has parts that mesh. The difference, which is the whole of the trick, is that watches do not leave a trail of almost-watches that died, and finches do. The trail is the notebook. Skip the notebook and you will hire a craftsman every time. Keep the notebook and the craftsman becomes a mesh you can miss: a pile of deaths that would have looked, from inside any one of them, like a plan that failed.

He did not start with the trick. He started with a notebook. Islands. Barnacles. Pigeons in a shed. A clergyman’s son who could sit still and then refuse the cheap sentence. The beaks were not a revelation in a dream. They were a pile of measurements that would not sit still under “each kind was issued.” Malthus gave him a rate. Breeders gave him a proof that selection can be hurried by a hand. The conclusion — descent with a mesh — was keen because the observations were allowed to kill the other story. A man on the other side of the world, looking at beetles and islands of his own, reached the same mesh without asking permission. That is what this book means by a path: **a record you could have missed, then a sentence that can die.** The leftover glow is that habit aimed at a fog. The dark sea is that habit aimed at a lid of ice. A craftsman is what you hire when you skip the notebook.

The man on the other side was Wallace. Same mesh, different kitchen, a letter from the Malay Archipelago that arrived in time to make the announcement a joint one. That matters for the method, not for a statue. Two notebooks, two worldlines, one sentence that could have been wrong and was not. Malthus had only meant a sermon about the poor. The rate he named — more mouths than food, if nothing checks — is a filter when you let it run on beaks. Breeders had only meant prettier pigeons. The shed is a proof that a mesh, hurried by a hand, can make a pouter from a rock dove in a career. Nature’s hand is slower and has no taste. The taste is what dies.

![Figure 42. One Galápagos finch on a light ground, beak readable. A filter can look like a craftsman.](Figures/figs/fig42.jpg)

The cosmic version is ruder because we cannot see the deaths. If there are dead valleys, they do not send baby pictures. If there are dead tiles, they are out of reach. If there are dead branches, they do not radio. We see a leftover glow that permits spirals, a shove that waited, a nuclear force that permits carbon, a Jupiter that squeezes a moon until a sea stays wet. The craftsman story is the cheap story. The filter story is the one that does not invent a someone.

Hot: this universe permits chemists. Warm: some of the coincidences of Chapter 41 are what a cut looks like, if the draws exist. Cold: a someone numbered the leftover glow. The cold claim has had centuries of poetry and no channel. Beauty is not a serial number. A blue marble is not a signature. A dark sea is a humility, not a second signature. You may still find the glow beautiful. Finding it beautiful is a fact about a worldline. It is not a telegram.

Anthroposophy — a spiritual cosmos arranged around human becoming — is a craftsman story with incense. It does not survive the leftover glow, the three minutes, or the shove. It is not a warm claim that failed. It is a different game. This book does not play it.

Steiner’s cosmos has a plot: human spirit as the point of the loaf, epochs, a moral weather in the planets. Plots are allowed in novels. Plots that must survive the leftover helium have to predict the helium, or at least not contradict the workshop that made it. Anthroposophy does not fail a nuclear test because it does not sit the test. It does not fail the shove because it does not use the bombs. It is not in the temperature system. It is a different kitchen, and this book’s kitchen is the one with a clock that already lied. You may keep the incense if the incense is how you stay kind. Do not promote it to a thermal history.

Where, then, is God?

If the word means a person who numbered the leftover glow, aimed a river kingdom’s stones, and poured a library into the first ocean — a colleague, larger — then the word is the watchmaker again. The channel nature does not use has not spoken. Beauty is not a serial number. This book will not pretend a gap in the first chemical hour is a detection.

That is the cold hire. Paley’s watch, scaled up. Chapter 34 already asked it to clock in: a leftover in the air, a serial number in the glow, a signal that is not the ionosphere. The glow’s freckles look like stretched noise. The stones of a river kingdom look like a people who could cut rock. The first ocean looks like chemistry that either copied or did not. A gap is not a someone. A gap is a gap. You may call the someone by an old name in a kitchen. The field equation will not countersign the name. Hot as a sociological fact: people hire the watchmaker. Cold as physics: the hire has not clocked in.

If the word means the reason there is a loaf rather than not, the question is honorable and unanswered. Naming the question is not a theory. It does not predict the helium fraction. It does not fix Λ. It does not open a handle. You may keep the name in a kitchen, the way you keep “now.” Do not promote it to a cosmology. Einstein’s habit, when he said God, was often the order itself — *Deus sive Natura*, a dignity for the metric, not a will. That habit is poetry with a good ear. It still does not give you a prayer that changes a worldline from outside the loaf.

Why is there a casserole rather than an empty oven? That is a kitchen sentence for the honorable question. Physics, as we have it, takes the loaf as given and asks how the slices relate. It is very good at that. It is silent, so far, on why there is dough. Silence is not a detection of a baker. Silence is silence. Barrow would have called the question an idea for ideas and then gone back to the leftover glow, which is a measurement. This book will do the same. You may sit with the question after you take the casserole out. Sitting with it is not a third temperature. It is a refusal to fake a result.

If the word means the laws, you have renamed the equations. Allowed. Empty.

Calling the Friedmann equation God does not make the stretch kinder. Calling the leftover glow a hymn does not change its temperature. Rename if the rename keeps you from treating the mesh as a someone. Do not imagine the rename added a will.

The devil is the same furniture with the sign flipped. A cosmic adversary who wants the host to lose is a craftsman of evil, and Chapter 30 already fired that hire: survival at all cost is a fitness peak, not a personality. Death keeps the score. Entropy is the arrow we borrowed from a smooth beginning, not a being who likes ashes. Suffering is real. It happens on threads. It does not become a second deity because we need someone to blame who is not the filter. You may use the old words for the old dread. The field equation will not countersign them.

The pointer is enough. Chapter 30 wrote the peak — the wasp, the nest, the invoice paid in other bodies — and this chapter will not rewrite it. The temptation, in a book that has just fired a cosmic craftsman of good, is to keep a cosmic craftsman of evil so the dread has a face. Faces are for kitchens. The mesh does not have one. A parasitoid is a local success, not a deputy. A plague is a copy that found a host, not a sermon. Entropy is Chapter 44’s arrow, a boundary condition, not a preference. If you need a word for the dread, use the dread. Do not staff a second throne.

What we *can* be is a local craftsman. The greenhouse is a beak we built for a seed we brought. The melt-probe is a beak for a dark sea. The library is a beak for a future dirt. That is not the universe arranging itself. That is a worldline arranging a room. The difference is the whole moral, if a cosmology book is allowed one: do not wait for the loaf to love you. Do not require the ending to compute you back. Grow the basil. Taste the plume. Send the machine. Admit that it may fail. Call that, if you need a word, meaning — and keep it off the gravitational field equation.

Local is the word that does the work. The woman hitting the airlock is a craftsman in a glove. The machine chewing ice is a craftsman that does not sleep. The box of sequences in Chapter 32 is a craftsman aimed at a later dirt. None of them is a clause in Einstein’s equation. None of them is required by the leftover helium. All of them are allowed by a permit — water, carbon, a little quiet, a launch window — and by a refusal to wait. Meaning, on this reading, is not scheduled at crunch and not denied by heat death. It is a project on a thread. Projects can fail. That is what keeps them off the list of destinies. Destiny was the thing that was not allowed.

Hot: there are worldlines that arrange rooms. Warm: that arrangement is the only craftsman this cosmology can sign. Cold: the loaf will notice and grade the basil. The loaf does not grade. The dirt does. The dirt can vote no.

Paley’s watch, found on a heath, was an argument for a someone. The greenhouse is a watch we can name the maker of. She hits the airlock. She logs the pH. The melt-probe is a watch with no one inside, chewing ice because a worldline decided to look. The library is a watch aimed at a later dirt. Those are craftsmen. They are local. They leave a trail of invoices, not a serial number in the leftover glow. That is the inversion the cosmic hire never survives: when you can name the hand, you have a project. When you cannot, you have a mesh or a sermon. This book will keep the project and fire the sermon.

---

\newpage

# PART X — What the Future Is Allowed to Be

---

\newpage

## 43. Five Ways It Can End

The leftover glow does not say how the story closes. The shove does most of the talking, and it talks in a low, accelerating voice. Here are five closings, with their temperatures.

A kitchen loaf can finish in more than one way. It can dry until it is a brick. It can be pulled apart while it is still warm. It can be shoved back into a hotter oven and collapse. It can spoil from the inside, a bubble of wrong chemistry that eats the crumb at the speed of the room. It can be sliced so thin and glued to tomorrow’s dough that you call the end a beginning. Only one of those is on the receipt you were handed with the ingredients. The receipt, in this cosmos, is the bombs, the leftover glow, and a shove that is winning. The receipt says: it dries. The other four are allowed as other kitchens, other valleys, other mistakes in how we read *w*. They are not on this ticket. Katie Mack wrote the tour; Barrow would have walked you to the door of each and then asked which one had a measurement. This chapter does that, with a clock.

**Heat death.** The working default. The stretch keeps winning. Bound islands last a long time and then do not: stars burn out, remnants cool, black holes leak (Chapter 23), the islands thin toward a sameness in which no heat can run a trick. Time, as a useful word for change, gets bored. This is Kelvin’s old gloom, updated with dark energy. It is not a crunch. It is a fade. Hot as the track the bombs picked. Warm only in the details no one has photographed.

William Thomson — Kelvin — said it in the nineteenth century, when the leftover glow was not yet a fact and the shove was not yet a bomb. Stars would burn out. The universe would reach a sameness of temperature. No heat engine, no trick, no kitchen. The gloom was thermodynamics applied to a cosmos he could not yet weigh. The update is the shove. Without it, you still had a fight: pull versus stretch, a possible recollapse, a possible tired forever. With it, the fight is over unless the shove is a liar. Isolated lumps stay bound — the Local Group, a while; a galaxy, longer. Between them the dough wins and keeps winning. New stars, after a long career, stop being made. Red dwarfs, the stingy ones, spend on the order of 10¹³ to 10¹⁴ years and then sit as cinders. White dwarfs, neutron stars, brown leftovers: a degenerate era, dimming. Then the holes. A stellar-mass hole leaks on a timescale of order 10⁶⁷ years. A giant, a billion Suns, can last toward 10¹⁰⁰. Chapter 23 already wrote the leak. After the last leak, the islands are a thin of particles and a leftover stretch, too even to run a trick. That is the fade. Hot as a default given the bombs. Warm in the details: we have not photographed year 10¹⁰⁰. We have the equations that got the leftover glow right, pointed forward. Mara, if the potatoes prosper, still lives inside one bound island. The island can go dark without a recollapse. The afternoon is long enough to grow basil.

**The rip.** If the shove is not a constant but a growing rudeness — *w* less than −1, phantom, a word that should not have survived the committee — then bound things unbind. Galaxies, then stars, then worlds, then you. Finite time. No evidence requires this. The bombs permit a constant. Cold, unless *w* misbehaves in a future survey.

*w* is the ratio of pressure to energy density. A cosmological constant sits at *w* = −1. Acceleration needs *w* less than −1/3. A rip needs the phantom: *w* less than −1, and no decay of the field that would let it sit down. Then the shove grows. Clusters unbind. Galaxies unbind. The Solar System unbinds. Atoms, in the rude versions, unbind. There is a last day, and it is not a metaphor. The working surveys, as of this kitchen, are consistent with *w* = −1 to a few percent. That is not a proof it cannot wander later. It is a proof it has not been required to. The working surveys, as of this kitchen, have not been required to make *w* phantom. If a future survey finds it, this paragraph gets a promotion.

**The crunch.** Pull wins. The web falls back. The last three minutes are a hot workshop in reverse, then a scramble, then a singularity or a bounce. The bombs and the leftover glow say we are not on this track unless the shove is a liar. Cold as destiny; allowed as a different valley.

A crunch was the textbook future for a long time, the one that made forever-minds look like a calculation. Chapter 19 already broke the calculation. The shove is winning. Recollapse needs the pull to take the budget back. It has not. Even in a universe that *did* crunch, the mixmaster of Chapter 16 is a scramble, not a control panel. BKL chaos is not a computer. You may still write a crunch as a different bubble’s fate. You may not write it as ours.

**Vacuum decay.** A jitter somewhere turns the vacuum into a ruder vacuum. A bubble of the new one grows at light, rewriting the laws inside it. No warning: the warning would arrive with the bubble. The leftover glow’s vacuum looks stable enough that we are still here. “Stable enough” is not “stable.” Cold-to-warm; do not lose sleep; do not pretend the risk is zero.

The Higgs vacuum, given the measured masses of the Higgs and the top quark, may be a metastable sit: not the lowest valley, a ledge. A ledge can tunnel. Coleman and De Luccia wrote the bubbles; the wall, once nucleated, runs at light. You would not see it coming because seeing is a light-speed fact, and the wall is that fact. The estimated lifetime, in the working Standard Model, is vastly longer than the present age — longer, often, than the fade. That is why you should not lose sleep. The estimate depends on numbers we still sharpen and on physics above the energies we have stacked. That is why you should not pretend the risk is zero. If a bubble arrives, there is no project that outruns it. Mara’s greenhouse, the melt, the library — all of them are inside the same vacuum.

**The bounce.** The end is a middle. A crunch becomes a bang, or a dark sea of extra-dimensional branes slaps, or Penrose’s conformal trick glues a thin future to a next beginning. Cyclic, ekpyrotic, conformal: names, papers, no leftover-glow signature that has stuck. Cold.

A bounce needs the pull to win first, or a different high-curvature theory that turns a bang into a rebound, or a pair of branes that collide in a room we have not photographed. Loop-quantum sketches bounce. Ekpyrotic sketches slap. Penrose’s conformal cyclic cosmology glues a thinned, far future to a next fog by a rescaling that makes infinity look like a start. Each of these is an idea for ideas with equations. None of them has a leftover-glow signature that has survived the surveys as a detection.

![Figure 43. Five rows: Freeze, Rip, Crunch, Decay, Bounce. One word each.](Figures/figs/fig43.png)

Notice what is not on the list: a required forever-mind. Notice what *is* on the list, quietly, as a project rather than a fate: a greenhouse, a dark sea, a machine that does not sleep. Those do not close the cosmos. They occupy a few more worldlines before the fade. That is the only closing this book will bless.

The Omega Point wanted a sixth row: crunch, then mind, then God. Chapter 19 deleted the row. The Final Anthropic Principle wanted the row as a law. The data did not countersign. You may still want mind to last. Wanting is a project. Put it in the greenhouse, in the probe, in the library. Do not put it in the Friedmann equation.

Appendix A43 writes *w*, decay rates, bounce conditions. Here, pick the default without romance: a long accelerating stretch, islands going dark, the leftover glow thinning until even the holes begin to leak. If you want a prettier last page, write it yourself, in a room, while there is still a room.

---

\newpage

## 44. Does Time Exist?

We have used “time” as if we knew.

In the kitchen, time is the clock. In relativity, time is a direction in the loaf, mixed with space, sliced differently by walkers. Proper time is a length of thread. The leftover glow picks out a useful slicing — cosmic time — because the glow is almost the same everywhere and we can watch it cool. That slicing is a convenience the universe approximately offers, not a throne.

The wall clock is still the right tool for a casserole. It is a terrible cosmology. GPS is the grown-up version of the same split. The satellites carry clocks. The clocks run fast, relative to the ground, because they sit higher in Earth’s well, and they run slow because they are moving. Both corrections are geometry. The well’s gift is the larger one: about forty-five microseconds a day of speeding-up. The motion’s tax is about seven microseconds a day of slowing-down. The net, about thirty-eight microseconds a day, would be a joke if light were slow. Light is not slow. Thirty-eight millionths of a second, times the speed of light, is on the order of ten kilometers of lie per day if you skip the metric. That is why the phone in the kitchen can find the street. You do not need a philosophy of time to land a plane. You need the interval. That is the first honest answer, and it is hot.

Mara and Eli are still on that argument, years apart. He is allowed to use the wall clock. Her radio is the late relation the clock cannot annex. Chapter 1 already gave them Andromeda as the thing the clock cannot settle. This chapter gives them GPS as the thing the geometry can. The clock is a local tool. The satellites are the same tool, corrected. Neither is a river. Both are relations among events: a tick here, a tick there, a radio that is late on purpose. If “does time exist?” is a way of asking whether those relations are real, the answer is yes, and your dinner depends on it.

Does time *exist*? The honest answers come in flavors.

**Yes, as geometry.** The loaf has a metric. Intervals are real. This is the hot answer, and it is enough to run GPS.

Enough, also, to run the twin who comes home younger, the particle that lasts longer in the lab, the radio that answers twenty minutes late. Chapters 1 through 6 spent this money. An event is a happening. The interval between happenings is the thing the geometry cares about. Proper time is how much of that interval a particular thread accumulates. “Does time exist?” in this flavor is like asking whether length exists. Length is a relation among events along a ruler. Time is a relation among events along a worldline. The relation is as real as the casserole’s doneness. The throne — a universal now the relations are painted on — is what the kitchen clock invented and relativity retired.

**Yes, as an arrow, but the arrow is a boundary.** The laws do not prefer a direction. The low-entropy past does. Eggs smash because the beginning was smooth. Carroll’s Past Hypothesis is this sentence in a better suit. If the past had been a scramble, we would not have a “we.”

The microscopic rules run as well backward as forward. That is not a rumor. It is a feature of the equations that got the three minutes right. The arrow you feel — memory, cause, the way a cup does not unshatter — is not a second law hiding in the metric. It is a fact about the region we call the past: it was special, smooth, low in the kind of mess that would have been a heat death at the start. From a special past, the typical futures are messier. That is the arrow. It is hot as a thermodynamic fact in kitchens and stars. It is warm as a cosmological claim: we do not have a derivation of *why* the past was special. We have the leftover glow as a photograph of a smoothness, and a name for the postulate that says the smoothness was there. The Past Hypothesis is that name. Without it you do not get observers who remember. With it you do not need a river.

Memory is the arrow made intimate. You remember the special past because the special past left records, and records are a kind of order: a photograph, a scar, a leftover glow, a notebook of beaks. You do not remember the fade because the fade has not left you a fossil of itself in your head. That is not a failure of the brain. It is what a boundary condition looks like from inside a thread. An egg smashes and does not un-smash for the same reason. The laws would allow the un-smash. The past would not. The kitchen already knew this before it had a name. Cosmology’s job was to notice that the leftover glow is the same kind of fact, written on the sky.

**Not as a river.** There is no glowing NOW moving down the loaf. Chapters 1 through 6 were that retirement.

A river would be a privileged slice, lit, advancing, making the future into the present and the present into the past. The loaf does not have that furniture. Different walkers slice differently. Andromeda’s navy is not waiting for your kitchen instant. The leftover glow offers a convenient slicing because it is almost the same everywhere, a last-scatter surface you can watch cool. Convenience is not a throne. You may still say “now” to take the casserole out. You should. Just do not promote the kitchen to a cosmology.

**Maybe not as a fundamental word.** In some attempts to quantize gravity, the wave of the universe does not mention time. The Wheeler–DeWitt equation is a constraint, a “this must add up,” not a “this happens next.” Julian Barbour takes the hint all the way: the inventory is configurations, Nows, a heap. Dynamics is what a heap looks like when it contains records of other heaps. Carlo Rovelli takes a different hint: time is what a system with heat says about itself. Thermal time. A useful word for a useful state.

Wheeler and DeWitt wrote a wavefunction of geometry that sits still: Ĥ Ψ = 0, no external *t* on the page. That is the problem of time in quantum gravity, and it is not a slogan. If the whole loaf is the system, there is no clock outside it to say “next.” Time, if it is to be recovered, has to be recovered from inside: a subsystem that can serve as a clock, a semiclassical limit where a geometry becomes classical enough to have a *t*, a relational fact among degrees of freedom. One family of recoveries says: treat part of the loaf as a clock and ask how the rest correlates with it. Another says: when the geometry is large and gentle, a WKB limit hands you something that looks like the Schrödinger equation in a *t* that was not fundamental. Warm-to-cold as a research program. No experiment has selected the recovery. The GPS clock does not adjudicate this fight. GPS lives in the limit where the recovery has already succeeded, or was never needed, because the metric is classical enough to land a plane.

Barbour’s heap is the austere version. The world is a pile of Nows — instantaneous configurations — and what we call motion is a correlation: this Now contains records that match that Now, the way a brain contains a memory that matches a room. There is no moving spotlight. There is a heap with internal likenesses. It is a picture that takes “no river” all the way to “no primitive duration.” It is cold. It is the right kind of cold: it does not add a someone, and it does not contradict GPS, which lives in the classical limit where the heap looks like a loaf.

A fossil, in the heap, is a Now that contains a record of another Now: a glow with a temperature, a notebook with a beak, a scar. Dynamics is the pattern of those containments. You do not need a spotlight moving down a hallway of rooms. You need the rooms to include pictures of other rooms. That is either a deep truth or a way of talking. We do not yet have a leftover-glow test that picks. What we have is a warning: do not smuggle a river back in just because English has a tense.

A tempting merger waits here, and Chapter 37 already taught the habit for refusing it. Barbour has a heap of Nows. Everett has a sum of branches. Both say the inventory is bigger than the slice you remember. Put them in one coat and you get a slogan: every possible Now exists, so the multiverse is just the heap. Two drawers, one bench.

Open them one at a time. The heap is configurations: every arrangement the stuff could have, one point per arrangement, whether or not anything in it remembers anything. That is configuration space, a map of the possible. Everett’s branches are not points on that map. A branch is a term in the wave that has decohered from the other terms, a pattern of records that agree with each other about which face the coin shows. Most points in the heap belong to no branch worth the name. They are scrambles with no records at all. A branch is a thin, well-behaved thread through the heap, picked out by decoherence. Not by time. Not by being possible.

Barbour did build a bridge. In his picture the wave of the universe piles its weight on the Nows that hold consistent records, which he calls time capsules. That is a bridge between two drawers, not a fusion of them. It is a proposal about where the weight sits, and nobody has derived it from anything we can test. The GPS clock is no help here either. It lives where the heap already looks like a loaf.

Hot: decoherence is a measured rate. Warm: Everett as a reading of the rule that builds chemistry. Cold: the heap as the fundamental inventory, and the time capsules as its weighting. Colder than any of them: the fused slogan. Possible is not the same word as realized. A map of every arrangement is not a census of worlds.

Rovelli’s thermal time is a different recovery. A system with enough heat, enough statistical mix, can have a flow defined by that mix: time as what equilibrium says about itself. In ordinary kitchens this is overkill. In a quantum gravity where no *t* is on the page, it is a candidate for where the word comes back. Cold-to-warm. Allowed. Not a photograph.

![Figure 44. A clock face dissolving into a pile of marks.](Figures/figs/fig44.png)

Hartle and Hawking’s no-boundary idea tries to replace “before the bang” with a rounded-off geometry, time crystallizing only when the universe is large enough to have it. Vilenkin tunnels from nothing, which is a technical nothing, not a kitchen nothing. These are cold, and they are the right kind of cold: they are attempts to stop using a word where the word may not apply.

“Before the fog” may not be a kitchen word. Empty, in physics, is already a something: a vacuum, a rule, a jitter. A technical nothing is a path integral without a prior boundary, or a tunneling from a state that is not a waiting room. Neither is an empty oven with the lights off. Chapter 7 already refused the waiting room. This chapter refuses to staff it with a *t*. Cold: we do not have a leftover-glow fossil of the rounding-off. Allowed: the attempt is what you do when you take the problem of time seriously instead of drawing a throne and calling it God.

This book’s working answer, the one that can carry a greenhouse and a dark sea: time exists as relations among events. It does not exist as a stage the events are put down on. It may not exist, in the quantum-gravity limit, as a primitive. The arrow exists because the past was special. You exist as a thread that remembers the special past and cannot remember the fade. That is enough to hit an airlock and call it morning.

The wall clock still useful, not a cosmology. That is the kitchen sentence. Use it to take the casserole out. Use GPS to know where the greenhouse is. Use proper time to know how old the woman is when the window opens. Use cosmic time as a convenience when you want to say how long after the leftover glow’s last scatter a galaxy formed. Do not use any of them as a river the loaf is floating on. Do not use “before” as a room. Do not use a required forever-mind as a clock that runs after the holes have leaked. Relations among events are enough. They were always enough. The rest was a throne we built because kitchens have clocks and clocks have a way of looking like laws.

Hot: intervals, GPS, the arrow as a fact about a smooth past. Warm: the Past Hypothesis as the name of that smoothness; cosmic time as a convenience. Cold: a primitive *t* in the quantum-gravity wave; a waiting room labeled BEFORE; a river. The working answer sits in the hot and the warm and lets the cold stay cold. That is how this book has used temperatures since the front. It will not stop at the last chapter but one.

---

\newpage

## 45. The Honest Ending

Let us cash the recognitions, the way a book of this family is supposed to. Cashing is what you can *do* once the recognition has arrived and stayed. A second reading of the same list would be a choir.

An event is a happening: so you stop asking where the casserole “is” in time and ask which happenings it is between. “Now” is a slice you draw: so you keep the word for the kitchen and retire it for Andromeda. The past we can rebuild is a region: so the three minutes are not a myth you are being asked to respect, they are a workshop whose tools you still own. Most of what pulls does not shine: so you weigh by lean, not by glow. Most of the budget shoves: so the future is a fade unless the shove is a liar, and a required last mind is a story. A horizon is a one-way fact: so you do not book a door. A handle is a bill: so you do not staff a subway. Copies are four kinds: so you do not fuse them and then sell a ticket. Arrangement is a filter: so you do not crown the fish. Time is a relation: so the wall clock stays a tool. Each of those is a refusal that leaves a project standing. The project is the point of the cash.

And then the part the leftover glow does not owe us:

A dead world can hold a greenhouse if someone bullies the ice and the hours. A frozen moon can hold a sea if a giant squeezes it, and that sea can hold a second origin or an empty kitchen, and the only way to know is to melt and look, without poisoning the answer. A crew that does not sleep can carry a library farther than flesh. The library is a physical object. The project can fail. Failure is allowed. Destiny was the thing that was not allowed.

That paragraph is the second trunk of the book, and it is not a schedule of downtowns. It is a permit list. Water, carbon, a little quiet, a launch window, tact. No downtown assigned. No recovery of this world promised by leaving it. No helium for a body that wants a thousand centuries. Those rooms, if they are rooms, are later kitchens. This cosmology signs a smaller invoice: a greenhouse that may die in month four, a melt that may taste an empty, a library that may miss. The invoice is interesting *because* it can fail. Destiny cannot fail. That is why destiny was the thing that was not allowed.

![Figure 45. Rover tracks toward a near horizon. Meaning is local, on one worldline.](Figures/figs/fig45.jpg)

If every allowed outcome is on the inventory, this outcome is still the one with your hands in it. If the block is real, the basil you log on a cold morning is as real as the leftover helium. If the block is not quite real, if time is a heap of Nows, the heap still includes the smell of a lie in a room of LEDs. You do not need the universe to have a purpose in order to have a purpose in it. You need a permit — water, carbon, a little quiet — and a refusal to wait for the ending to compute you back.

Local, on one worldline. That is the moral the field equation will not write and does not have to. Everett, if you buy the reading, puts other outcomes on the inventory. The cheap copies, if space is that large, put other kitchens out of reach. Neither of them cooks this basil. Grief is still a fact about one thread. So is a project. So is the decision not to plague a dark sea. Meaning that had to be scheduled at crunch was a way of not having to decide. Meaning that is local is a way of having to.

Is there a path to ultimate knowledge?

There is a path. There is no chapel at the end of it, and the word *ultimate* is a temptation the loaf itself refuses.

Knowledge, in this book, is a record you could have missed. A prediction that can fail. A fossil that does not need your agreement: the glow, the helium, the stretch, the ring around a hole, a leftover in a navy file that still has no name. The path is the widening of that kind of record — not a feeling, not a collapse of the ocean when you look, not a framework that can survive every outcome and therefore explains none.

Darwin’s notebook was already the whole method. A pile of beaks. A sentence that could die. Wallace’s letter. The leftover glow is that habit aimed at a fog. Weinberg’s three minutes are that habit aimed at a workshop. The bombs of 1998 are that habit aimed at a future that did not want to recollapse. A melt that tastes a sea is that habit aimed at a lid of ice. The path does not change species when the subject changes. It does not become a chapel when the subject is the loaf. It stays a record you could have missed. Lengthen it. Do not crown it. Do not build a last room and call the room God.

The steps are few, and they are severe. They are also old. A notebook, a pile of beaks, a sentence that can die: that is already the whole method. The sky is a larger shed of pigeons.

Learn the bills. Special relativity: no shared now, no local dash past light, mail that is late on purpose. General relativity: gravity is the loaf; a handle and a bubble are sentences that still owe a stuff we have not stacked. Quantum mechanics: amplitudes, decoherence, pairings without a telegram. The seam where the two great theories do not yet share a kitchen is the largest honest hole we have. Sit in it. Do not furnish it with a visitor or a god of the gaps.

The bills are not homework for a degree. They are the price of not being fooled. Special relativity is why Andromeda’s navy is a parable and not a paradox. General relativity is why a horizon is a fact about events, why GPS needs a metric, why a handle has an invoice. Quantum mechanics is why a pairing is not a radio and why a branch, if Everett is right, is not a ticket. The seam is the hole where those two great theories do not yet agree on a kitchen: a geometry that is also a quantum object, a time that may not be a primitive, a bang that may not have a before. Sitting in the seam is the opposite of filling it. Filling it is how you hire a craftsman. Sitting in it is how you keep the next record honest. **String theory cannot currently be tested.** It is one of the attempts to furnish the seam. It has not named a kill-shot. Until it does, it is a large, ingenious maybe. Maybes are allowed. Maybes are not results.

Learn the walls. An event horizon is a fact about influence. A cosmic event horizon is a fact about which galaxies will never get your letter. A branch, if Everett is right, is a fact about a term you cannot enter. “Before the fog” may not be a word. Those are not insults. They are the shape of what a worldline is allowed to know.

Walls are not dares. A dare is a poster that says the wall is a door if you are brave. Chapter 24 already fired the poster. You cannot fetch a letter from inside a hole. You cannot fetch a letter from a galaxy past the cosmic event horizon. You cannot hop a branch. You cannot sit in a waiting room labeled BEFORE and interview the fog. Knowing the walls is part of the path. It keeps you from spending a life on a ticket that was never for sale. It also keeps you from despair: the rooms that *are* in your future cone — a dead world with ice in its bones, a moon with a sea, a dish that can hear a shout — are not small because they are not the whole loaf. They are the part of the loaf a thread can still work.

Refuse what cannot die. A story that fits every leftover glow is not a story. String theory, as it stands, has not named a kill-shot. A craftsman who leaves no channel nature does not use has not clocked in. An observation-creates-life that cannot say what counted as the first observer is a regress, not a path.

The refusal is a temperature rule. If a claim cannot be wrong, it is not a claim in this kitchen. The leftover glow could have been a different temperature; it was not. The helium could have been a different fraction; it was not. The stretch could have been slowing; it is not. Those are sentences that could have died. Keep them. Fire the ones that survive every outcome.

Build the next record. A telescope that can smell an oxygen-methane pair and be wrong. A dish that can hear a shout and know the shout was not the ionosphere. A melt that tastes a dark sea without writing over it. A library that is a box, not a sermon. A legislature, even, when it is doing its only smart job: asking whether the leftover in its own air is an adversary, a secret of its own, or a file that still says unidentified. That last is not ultimate knowledge. It is hygiene.

Melt, dish, library: three beaks. The melt is the only way a second origin, or a clean empty, leaves this solar system as a measurement instead of a mood. The dish is the only way a shout that is not us becomes a fossil. The library is the only way a trick of carbon and water travels farther than a body that has to eat. None of them is a destiny. Each of them can fail. Each of them is a widening of the record. Planetary protection is the tact that keeps the melt from becoming a flood. Chapter 32 already wrote the rule: look first, seed later, never confuse the two. Tact is not a chapel. Tact is how a local craftsman does not become a plague.

What would *ultimate* have to mean, if we kept the word? Not a God’s-eye inventory of every branch, every before, every event outside our cone. That inventory, if it exists, is not a destination. Ultimate, for a thread, is asymptotic: more of the loaf brought into a record that can fail, fewer sermons in the gaps, a closing of the QM–GR seam if the seam can be closed, a second dictionary under ice or a clean empty, a leftover named or honestly unnamed. You will not finish. The fade, if the fade is coming, will not wait until you finish. The point of the path is not to arrive at a last fact. It is to be the kind of worldline that can tell a last fact from a mood.

Lengthen without a chapel. That is the whole close, if you want it in four words. A chapel is a last room that cannot be wrong. This cosmology has no such room. It has a kitchen clock that is still useful, a leftover glow that is still late, a shove that is still winning, a seam that is still a hole, and a handful of projects that still might fail. That is a richer inventory than a throne. It is also a ruder one. Rudeness is the courtesy of a universe that does not need us in order to have had a first three minutes, and does not owe us a last mind in order for a morning to be worth hitting an airlock.

The clock on the kitchen wall is still lying. Let it. Take the casserole out. Then, if you have the years and the machines and the tact not to plague a dark sea, send a library. Not because the loaf requires it. Because you can, and because the fade, if the fade is coming, will not be hurried or helped by your waiting.

That is the honest ending. It is not a door. It is a door you build, on a world that did not ask for one, under a sky that is late on purpose. It is also the only path this cosmology can sign: **widen the record, pay the bills, stop at the walls, and do not hire a someone to finish your homework.**

Meaning, if you still want the word after forty-five chapters, is local, expensive, and optional. It lives on one worldline, in a room that smells like a lie, under a clock that was never a cosmology. Grow the basil. Log the pH. Melt if you can do it without writing over the answer. Send the box if you have the tact. Admit that the dirt can vote no. That admission is not despair. It is the difference between a project and a destiny, and it is the last recognition this book will cash.

\newpage

# APPENDIX — The Scientific Detail

\newpage

## How to Read These Notes

Each note A*n* twins popular chapter *n*. Units are SI unless stated. *c* is the speed of light, *G* Newton’s constant, *ħ* the reduced Planck constant, *k* Boltzmann’s constant. A scale factor *a(t)* is dimensionless; today *a₀ = 1*. Cosmological redshift *z* obeys *1 + z = 1/a* for light emitted at *a*. A gravitational redshift, as in Pound–Rebka, is a different relation.

String theory, whenever it appears: a mathematical framework that **cannot currently be tested**. It has not produced a unique, risky prediction that would kill the whole idea if the experiment came out otherwise.

These notes are denser than the chapters they twin. They are still this book: short sentences, named measurements, temperatures on the claim. They are not a textbook. If a formula is numbered (1)–(17), the number is a handle, not a homework set. Row (18) is a standing rule, not a formula.

---


\newpage

## A0. Three Temperatures of Claim

**Hot:** repeated measurement, used to predict new ones. **Warm:** best model, real gaps. **Cold:** allowed by the equations, not selected by the data. A claim can change temperature. The oven is the sky, not the seminar.

Inflation is the cleanest change-of-temperature in this book. In 1981 Guth’s paper was a proposal: a brief early burst of repulsive stretch that could solve the horizon, flatness, and monopole problems the hot bang, taken alone, cannot. The first (“old”) inflation stalled on how to end the burst without emptying the universe of matter. In 1982 Albrecht–Steinhardt and Linde supplied slow-roll exits. That was still a model. What warmed the *local* burst was later data: a sky that is spatially flat to a percent; a primordial spectrum with *n_s* a few percent below 1 (A14); acoustic peaks in the leftover glow that match a nearly scale-invariant twitch stretched outside the Hubble radius and then brought back. Those are not proofs that a particular inflaton exists in a detector. They are reasons the local burst is no longer a 1981 maybe.

A claim can also cool. In March 2014 BICEP2 announced *B*-mode polarization at degree scales and offered a tensor-to-scalar ratio *r ~ 0.2*. Primordial gravitational waves from a high-scale burst would have been a trophy. Within a year Planck’s dust maps and the joint BICEP/Keck–Planck analyses showed that Galactic dust, not a primordial tensor background, carried the signal. The measurement was real. The interpretation was not. Current combinations (BICEP/Keck plus Planck) put *r ≲ 0.03–0.04* at 95% confidence, depending on the data cut. High-scale inflation of the 2014 headline is colder than it was on announcement day. The local burst is still warm. Eternal inflation — self-reproducing pocket nucleations, a measure problem, no unique risky number — did not inherit the warming. It stays allowed and unselected.

Temperature is a discipline, not a mood. “We have a picture of a black-hole shadow” is hot. “The interior is a bounce into a baby universe” is cold. String landscapes, when named, inherit the standing rule (18). Use the labels in that spirit for the rest of these notes.

---

\newpage

## A1. Relativity of Simultaneity

Einstein synchronization: a clock at spatial coordinate *x* is set by a light signal from a master clock, assuming the one-way speed is *c*. That assumption is a convention that special relativity makes consistent. Observers with relative velocity *v* along *x* then disagree on which events with Δ*x* ≠ 0 are simultaneous. The desynchronization is, to first order in *v/c*,

*Δt = v Δx / c²*.

This is not a signal. Nothing travels from here to Andromeda in the instant you take a step. It is a fact about how two inertial frames slice the same loaf.

Worked numbers, Andromeda at Δ*x*/c ≈ 2.5 × 10⁶ yr. Walking, *v ≈ 1.4 m/s*, so *v/c ≈ 4.7 × 10⁻⁹*. Then *Δt ≈ 4.7 × 10⁻⁹ × 2.5 × 10⁶ yr ≈ 0.012 yr ≈ 4 days*. The kitchen walker and the kitchen stander do not share a “now” on Andromeda to better than a long weekend. An airliner, *v ≈ 250 m/s*, tilts the same slice by *~2 yr*. Earth’s orbital speed, *v ≈ 30 km/s*, tilts it by *~250 yr*. None of those numbers is a photograph of Andromeda’s navy. Light from M31 is 2.5 million years late for everyone in the kitchen. The disagreement is about which events on that galaxy you *label* simultaneous with the casserole, not about which photons you receive.

At laboratory distances the same formula is tiny. Across a 10 m kitchen at walking speed, *Δt ~ 5 × 10⁻¹⁷ s* — below any clock you own. Relativity of simultaneity is therefore easy to miss and easy to oversell. The one-way speed used to set the clocks is the convention. Once both kitchens have adopted it, the disagreement about which distant events share a slice is not a convention. It is what the Lorentz transformation does to those clocks. Chapter 1’s lamps are that second fact. Calling the whole disagreement “only a convention” is the reading Chapter 1 refused. The disagreement becomes a plot point only when *Δx* is huge or *v* is not small. Chapter 1’s kitchen is the first case.

---

\newpage

## A2. Minkowski Coordinates and the Interval

An event is a point in a 4-manifold. In inertial coordinates the interval is

**(1)**  *ds² = −c² dt² + dx² + dy² + dz²*

*(signature convention −+++).*

*ds² < 0* timelike, *= 0* lightlike, *> 0* spacelike. *ds²* is invariant: every inertial observer computes the same number. The split into *dt* and *dℓ* is not. That is the entire content of “the loaf has a geometry; the slices are ours.”

Signature is a convention. Particle physicists often write *+−−−*, so the inequality for “timelike” flips. The physics does not. This book keeps −+++ so that spatial distances look like school geometry and proper time satisfies *c² dτ² = −ds²* along a worldline.

A number. Two sparks in a lab: Δ*t* = 5.00 μs, Δ*x* = 1.00 km, Δ*y* = Δ*z* = 0. Light would need 3.34 μs to cover that kilometer, so the pair is timelike. Equation (1) gives *ds² / c² = −(5.00 × 10⁻⁶)² + (10³ / c)² ≈ −1.38 × 10⁻¹¹ s²*. The proper time between them, for the inertial traveler who visits both, is *Δτ = √(−ds²)/c ≈ 3.72 μs*. A different inertial frame can assign 6 μs and a shorter Δ*x*; it cannot change *Δτ*. Swap the numbers to Δ*t* = 2.00 μs and the same kilometer and *ds² > 0*: spacelike. No traveler visits both. Different frames can disagree on order.

Null intervals are the paths of light in vacuum. They are the boundary of A3’s cones. If you take nothing else from this note, take invariance: the interval is the thing the universe keeps. Your clock and your ruler are how you spend it.

---

\newpage

## A3. Light Cones and Lookback Time

**(2)**  *ds² = 0*  ⇒  *dℓ = c dt*

The past light cone of an event *p* is the set of events that can signal to *p*. The future cone is the set *p* can signal to. Everything else is elsewhere: real, and unavailable as a cause or an effect of *p*. Equation (2) is the generators of those cones in inertial coordinates. In a curved, expanding cosmology the same idea survives: null geodesics still bound causal contact; the scale factor *a(t)* changes how far a null ray gets.

Lookback time to redshift *z* is the integral of *dt = da / (a H(a))* from *a = 1/(1+z)* to *a = 1*. It is not “distance over *c*” except as a slogan. In a matter-plus-Λ fit with *H₀ ≈ 67–73 km s⁻¹ Mpc⁻¹*, light emitted at *z = 1* has been traveling *~7.8 Gyr*; at *z = 2*, *~10.5 Gyr*; at last scattering *z_* ≈ 1090, the travel time is the age of the universe minus *~3.8 × 10⁵ yr*. The leftover glow is a baby picture with the baby long gone. The Sun in your sky is eight minutes old; Jupiter tonight is *~30–50 min* old depending on opposition; M31 is 2.5 Myr old in photons.

The night sky is the past cone, sampled. Astronomy is the only time travel we have: we receive news that already lies to our past. You cannot answer the news. You can wait for later news from a later event on the same worldline. Chapter 3’s lateness is geometry, not an instrument limit.

---

\newpage

## A4. Tilting the Simultaneity Slice

**(3)**  *t′ = γ (t − v x / c²)*

*where γ = 1 / √(1 − v²/c²).*

A plane *t′ = const* is tilted in *(t, x)*. The slope is *v/c²*. At kitchen *v* the tilt is invisible on kitchen *x* and enormous on megaparsec *x* (A1). The Andromeda paradox is this tilt: two people who meet in a kitchen, one walking, assign different “present” events to a galaxy 2.5 million light-years away. They do not assign different photons in the eyepiece.

Reality-transfer arguments (Rietdijk 1966; Putnam 1967) take the tilt as a metaphysical conveyor: if event *A* is present-for-me and event *B* is present-for-you-when-you-share-*A*, then *B* is already real-for-me. The chain is then run around the loaf until every event is “already real.” The Lorentz transformation in (3) is not on trial. The extra premise is: “present ⇒ real,” plus transitivity of that privilege across observers who do not share a global plane. This book rejects the privilege of the plane. The manifold plus metric is the inventory. A slice is a bookkeeping surface. Calling the slice “the now” does not promote it to a moving spotlight.

At *v* not small, *γ* matters. Equation (3) is the exact boost, not the first-order slogan. For GPS satellites (*v ≈ 3.9 km/s*, *γ − 1 ≈ 8.5 × 10⁻¹¹*) the *vx/c²* term is a daily desynchronization you must correct or the navigation solution drifts by kilometers (A5). For a *0.1 c* cruise, *γ ≈ 1.005*; for *0.9 c*, *γ ≈ 2.3*. The twin effect (A5) is the integral of proper time along two different worldlines that meet twice. The Andromeda tilt is what those worldlines do to *distant* labels between the meetings.

---

\newpage

## A5. Proper Time and the Twin Effect

Proper time along a timelike worldline: *c² dτ² = −ds²*. Between two meetings, the inertial path maximizes *τ*. That is the twin “paradox”: the accelerated twin has the smaller elapsed proper time. There is no paradox in the geometry. The worldlines are different lengths. The traveling twin is not inertial for the whole trip; the stay-at-home twin is (idealized). They meet twice and compare wristwatches. The comparison is a scalar. Everyone agrees.

Hafele–Keating (October 1971; *Science* 1972) flew commercial airliners around the world with cesium clocks and compared them to a ground set. Eastbound, Earth-rotation velocity adds to the jet; westbound it subtracts. Predicted: eastbound *−40 ± 23 ns*, westbound *+275 ± 21 ns* (special-relativistic plus gravitational pieces together). Measured: *−59 ± 10 ns* and *+273 ± 7 ns*. The signs and the hundreds of nanoseconds are the result. A classroom “clocks are metaphysical” speech does not survive that table.

Muons. Mean proper lifetime *τ₀ ≈ 2.20 μs*. Atmospheric muons are born *~10–20 km* up at *v* so close to *c* that *γ* is typically *~10–20*. Without dilation they would decay in a few hundred meters of flight; they reach the basement. Storage-ring measurements (CERN muon storage ring; later *g−2* rings) hold *γ ≈ 29.3* and find the lab lifetime *γ τ₀* to parts in a thousand. That is the twin effect with a particle that does not file a flight plan.

GPS. Orbit *v ≈ 3.9 km/s* gives a special-relativistic slowing *~7 μs/day*. The weaker gravitational potential at altitude gives a general-relativistic speeding *~46 μs/day*. Net: the satellite clock runs *~38–39 μs/day* fast versus a geoid clock if you do not correct. Light travels 11 km in 38 μs. Uncorrected, the navigation solution is junk in hours. Both corrections are tens of microseconds per day and opposite in sign, as the chapter said. The system is an industrial twin-plus-well clock.

---

\newpage

## A6. Causal Structure: Past, Future, Elsewhere

At an event *p*, *I⁻(p)* is the chronological past (events that can reach *p* on a future-timelike curve), *I⁺(p)* the chronological future, and the remainder of the manifold, minus the light-cone boundary, is spacelike-separated “elsewhere.” Causal past and future *J±(p)* include the null generators. These are the verbs the loaf actually has. “Now” is not among them.

A Cauchy surface is a spacelike 3-surface that intersects every inextendible causal curve exactly once. Data on a Cauchy surface determine the solution in the domain of dependence. Presentism, as a physics claim rather than a mood, wants a preferred Cauchy surface that is “the present,” advancing. General relativity does not supply a preferred one. Different foliations are different bookkeeping. In Minkowski space every inertial *t = const* is a Cauchy surface; none is privileged by the metric. In generic GR, global hyperbolicity may fail (Cauchy horizons; A35). Where it holds, you still have a stack of surfaces, not a glowing edge.

The block — the 4-manifold plus metric as the inventory — is the default reading once you accept (1) and the Einstein equation as the dynamics of that metric. It is not a proof that “the future is already filmed” in a fatalist sense. It is a refusal to add a moving spotlight the equations do not contain. Local becoming, if you want the word, is along a worldline: proper time (A5). It is not a cosmic weather front.

---

\newpage

## A7. FLRW: No Center, No Edge

The Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker metric assumes homogeneity and isotropy on spatial slices: the same density and the same expansion rate at every comoving point, no preferred direction. In coordinates comoving with the cosmic fluid,

*ds² = −c² dt² + a(t)² [dχ² + f_K(χ)² dΩ²]*,

where *K = +1, 0, −1* labels spatial slices that are *S³*, *E³*, or *H³* (closed, flat, open), and *f_K* is *sin χ*, *χ*, or *sinh χ*. There is no privileged center on those slices any more than there is a center of the Earth’s surface. Every galaxy can think of itself as at rest in the Hubble flow. “Expansion into what?” is a category error: *a(t)* rescales proper distances *between comoving observers* on the slices. The manifold is not required to sit in a higher-dimensional room with a spare radial direction labeled “outside.”

Observationally, the leftover glow is isotropic to *~10⁻⁵* after the dipole is removed (A9). Galaxy surveys (2dF, SDSS, DESI) find homogeneity on scales *≳ 100–300 Mpc*, with the usual argument about whether a given catalog is large enough. Spatial curvature *Ω_k* is consistent with 0 at the percent level or better in Planck-like fits (A11). Flat and infinite is allowed. Flat and finite (a 3-torus) is allowed and not selected. Closed with a large radius is allowed. None of those options puts you at the middle of a bomb.

The “bang” is a hot, dense *state* of the slices in the past, not a point in a pre-existing box. At *a → 0* the classical description fails (A44). That failure is not a location you can visit.

---

\newpage

## A8. Hubble–Lemaître Law and Redshift

**(4)**  *v = H₀ d*  (low *z*);  *1 + z = 1/a_emit*

*H₀* is *ȧ/a* today. Equation (4)’s velocity form is a local linearization: recession *v ≈ H₀ d* for *z ≪ 1*, where *d* is proper distance now and *v* is not a rocket velocity through a box. The exact observable is redshift. Photons stretch with the slice: a feature emitted at wavelength *λ_em* when the scale factor was *a_emit* arrives at *λ_obs = λ_em / a_emit*, hence *1 + z = 1/a_emit*. At *z = 1* the universe was half its present scale. At *z = 9* it was a tenth. At *z_* ≈ 1090 it was about a thousandth. Do not confuse *z* with lookback time; A3’s integral is the conversion, and it depends on the whole *H(z)* (A18).

Measured *H₀* still tensions. Planck-like early-universe fits (CMB acoustic scale plus the working ΛCDM inventory) give *H₀ ≈ 67.4 ± 0.5 km s⁻¹ Mpc⁻¹*. Late-universe distance ladders (Cepheids to Type Ia; SH0ES) give *H₀ ≈ 73 ± 1 km s⁻¹ Mpc⁻¹*. The gap is several times the quoted errors and has persisted through independent rungs and through *Gaia* parallaxes. That is a real problem for the working model, not a reason to deny expansion. Tip-of-the-red-giant-branch ladders and some strong-lens time delays sit in between or on one side or the other; they have not closed the case. This book treats *H₀* as measured twice, in tension, both measurements hot as measurements, the reconciliation warm-to-open.

Hubble’s original 1929 slope was *~500 km s⁻¹ Mpc⁻¹* and a universe too young for the rocks. The law was right; the calibration was not. The present tension is not that story again until someone shows which calibration, or which early-universe assumption, is the 1929 error. The law is not a center. It is *a(t)* made local.

---

\newpage

## A9. The CMB as a Blackbody

FIRAS on COBE (Mather et al.; Fixsen 2009 compilation) measured the sky-averaged spectrum as a blackbody at

*T₀ = 2.72548 ± 0.00057 K*.

Residuals from a Planck function are at the level of tens of parts per million. That is the most precise blackbody in nature we have ever taken apart. A blackbody needs a thermal history: the photons were in equilibrium with a charged plasma, then decoupled, then free-streamed while *T ∝ 1/a* (8). A “tired light” or “stars in a fog” story does not produce this spectrum plus the acoustic-peak pattern.

The dipole, *ΔT ≈ 3.4 mK*, is our motion relative to the rest frame of the glow: *v ≈ 370 km/s* toward Leo (more carefully, toward galactic coordinates near *l ≈ 264°, b ≈ 48°*). Subtract that and the intrinsic anisotropies are *ΔT/T ~ 10⁻⁵* — tens to hundreds of microkelvin. Those are the seeds of A14, printed on the last-scattering surface.

Last scattering: hydrogen recombination drops the free-electron fraction and the Thomson optical depth *τ* falls through 1. Planck-like fits put the peak of the visibility function at *z_* ≈ 1090 (1089.80 ± 0.23 in the 2018 TT,TE,EE+lowE+lensing chain; the chapter’s 1090 is the right digit). The temperature then was *T_* = *T₀ (1 + z_*) ≈ 2970 K*, which this book rounds to *3000 K*. Age at last scattering: *≈ 380,000 yr*. Helium recombination is earlier and less visible. Reionization at *z ~ 6–9* puts a small extra *τ* on the large-scale polarization; it is not a second fireball.

Hot: the spectrum, the dipole as motion, the *10⁻⁵* map, last scattering at *z ~ 10³*. Warm: the detailed recombination code and the optical-depth tail. Cold: a craftsman who painted the blackbody (A34).

---

\newpage

## A10. Thermal History, Minute by Minute

**(8)**  *T ∝ 1/a*  (photon temperature after e⁺e⁻ annihilation)

Equation (8) is the leftover glow’s thermometer once electron–positron annihilation has dumped its entropy into the photons (a factor *~ (11/4)^{1/3}* relative to the neutrinos). Before that, *T* still tracks *1/a* up to *g_** changes when species drop out of the bath. A radiation-dominated clock reads *t ~ 1 / T²* in natural units: hotter is younger.

A prose timeline, not a wallpaper. *t ~ 10⁻⁴³ s*, *T* at the Planck energy *~10³² K*: not a measurement; the classical metric is not to be trusted. A local inflationary burst, if it happened, ends somewhere around *t ~ 10⁻³⁶–10⁻³² s* in textbook sketches; *T_rh* after reheating is model-dependent (A13) and only required to sit above BBN. At *t ~ 10⁻⁶ s*, *T ~ 10¹³ K ~ 1 GeV*, the quark–gluon plasma hadronizes — inferred from heavy-ion experiments plus the cosmic expansion, not from a cosmic photograph. At *t ~ 0.01 s*, *T ~ 10¹¹ K ~ 10 MeV*: the chapter’s first rough mark; weak interactions still keep neutrinos and the *n/p* ratio in step. Neutrino decoupling: *t ~ 1 s*, *T ~ 1 MeV*. Electron–positron annihilation follows as *T* falls through *~0.5 MeV*, reheating the photons relative to the already-decoupled neutrinos. Big-Bang nucleosynthesis: *t ~ 1–3 min*, *T ~ 0.1–1 MeV* (below). Recess. Then a long wait. Recombination and last scattering: *t ~ 3.8 × 10⁵ yr*, *T ~ 3000 K*, *z_* ≈ 1090 (A9). The glow has been free-streaming since. Reionization by the first stars and nuclei: *z ~ 6–9*, a few hundred million years. Today: *T₀ = 2.725 K*, *t₀ ≈ 13.8 Gyr*.

Those marks are the working thermal history. They are not a filmed minute log. The ones with spectral or abundance teeth (BBN; the CMB) are hotter than the ones that are interpolation plus accelerator physics.

---

*Big-Bang Nucleosynthesis*

Neutron-to-proton freeze-out *n/p ≈ 1/6* when the weak rates fall behind the expansion at *T ~ 0.8 MeV*, then free decay (*τ_n ≈ 880 s*) toward *~1/7* by the time the deuterium bottleneck opens.

**(9)**  *Y_p ≈ 2 (n/p) / (1 + n/p) ≈ 0.25*

**(10)**  Deuterium bottleneck: D photodissociates until *T ≲ 0.1 MeV*.

Almost every neutron that survives is cooked into ⁴He. Equation (9) is why the helium mass fraction is a quarter and not a cosmic accident. Equation (10) is why the cooking waits three minutes: the bath is still full of gamma rays that break deuterium until *T* is low enough that *η × exp(−B_D / T)* drops, with baryon-to-photon ratio *η ≈ 6 × 10⁻¹⁰* from the CMB.

Observed *Y_p ≈ 0.24–0.25* (H II regions; primordial intercept). Deuterium, the most fragile useful thermometer, sits at *D/H ≈ 2.5 × 10⁻⁵* in high-*z* quasar absorbers, matching CMB *ω_b* at the ~10% level or better. ³He is messier (stars both make and destroy it). Lithium-7 remains in tension: Spite-plateau Population II stars show *A(Li)* a factor *~2–3* below the CMB-plus-BBN prediction. That is a real gap — stellar depletion, new physics, or both — not a reason to throw out *Y_p* and D/H. No detector has photographed the third minute. The abundances plus the expansion plus the weak rates are the courtroom.

---

\newpage

## A11. Critical Density and the Cosmic Inventory

**(5)**  *ρ_c = 3 H₀² / (8π G)*

*Ω_i = ρ_i / ρ_c*. For *H₀ = 67.4 km s⁻¹ Mpc⁻¹*, *ρ_c ≈ 8.5 × 10⁻²⁷ kg m⁻³* — a few hydrogen atoms per cubic meter if it were all hydrogen, which it is not. For *H₀ = 73* the critical density is ~17% higher; *Ω* values quoted from the CMB are usually reported in combinations like *Ω_i h²* that do not care which *H₀* you prefer until you convert.

Planck-like inventory (round numbers this book uses): *Ω_b ≈ 0.05*, *Ω_c ≈ 0.27*, *Ω_Λ ≈ 0.68*, *Ω_k ≈ 0*, photons *Ω_γ ~ 5 × 10⁻⁵*, neutrinos a comparable few × *10⁻⁵* if masses are small. Mean baryon number density *n_b ~ 0.2 m⁻³* (one proton in a box a bit more than a meter on a side). Stars and the gas you can photograph are a fraction of *Ω_b*; most baryons are in the warm–hot intergalactic medium and other faint phases. Dark matter *Ω_c* is the extra pull (A17). Dark energy *Ω_Λ* is the shove (A18). They do not trade costumes.

Spatial curvature: *|Ω_k| ≲ 0.01* in standard fits, tighter if you assume the working model. That is “flat for practical purposes,” not a proof the 3-space is infinite (A38). Critical density is the density that would make a matter-only universe spatially flat. With *Λ*, the Friedmann equation still uses (5) as the unit; the geometry is *Ω_k = 1 − Σ Ω_i*.

---

\newpage

## A12. Horizon, Flatness, and Relic Problems

Particle horizon in a radiation-dominated era grows as *~ ct*, more carefully *χ_p ~ 2ct / a* in comoving coordinates for *a ∝ t^{1/2}*. Opposite patches on the last-scattering surface, separated by ~1° or more on the sky today, had not been in causal contact in a pure hot-bang history. Yet the leftover glow agrees in temperature to *~10⁻⁵*. That is the horizon problem: a thermal agreement without a prior chance to thermalize.

Flatness: *|Ω − 1| ∝ 1/(a² H²)*. In decelerating eras (*radiation, matter*) this quantity *grows* toward the past, so *Ω ≈ 1* today to a percent requires *|Ω − 1| ≲ 10⁻¹⁶* at BBN and *~10⁻⁶⁰* near the Planck scale if you run the clock that far. That is a tuning if the bang is just a bang. It is automatic if a prior burst drives *aH* up exponentially and parks *Ω* near 1.

GUT-scale monopoles, if produced at a phase transition with *T ~ 10¹⁶ GeV*, freeze out with *n_M / s* large enough to overclose the universe by many orders unless they are never produced after a burst or are diluted by *≳ 10²⁵* in entropy. Other relics (domain walls, some moduli) tell similar stories. Inflation’s original sale was these three puzzles. The sale does not identify the inflaton (A13). It explains why a local burst was proposed, and why a burst that lasts *N ~ 50–60* e-folds (11) is the size of the proposal.

---

\newpage

## A13. Slow-Roll and E-Folds

**(11)**  *N = ∫ H dt ≈ 50–60* needed to solve A12.

An e-fold is a factor *e* in *a*. Fifty e-folds is a stretch of *e⁵⁰ ~ 5 × 10²¹*; sixty is *~10²⁶*. That is the dilution that flattens, homogenizes, and hides relics. Exact *N* depends on the reheating temperature and the scales you care about; 50–60 is the working window, not a sacred integer.

Slow-roll parameters *ε = −Ḣ/H²*, *η = ε̇/(Hε)* ≪ 1 (or the potential versions *ε_V = (M_Pl²/2)(V′/V)²*, *η_V = M_Pl² V″/V*). While they stay small the burst continues; when *ε* reaches 1, inflation ends. Reheating converts vacuum-like energy (*w ≈ −1*) into a radiation bath. *T_rh* can sit anywhere from a few MeV (below that, BBN is wrecked) up toward GUT scales in optimistic models. No inflaton has been identified in a detector. No laboratory field has been shown to be the one.

What warmed the *idea* is A14’s spectrum plus A9’s flatness, not a named particle. High-scale models that predicted *r* large enough for BICEP2’s first claim cooled when the dust was subtracted (A0). Starobinsky-like *R²* and a range of plateau potentials remain compatible with *n_s ≈ 0.96–0.97* and *r* below present bounds. The Lyth bound roughly says *r ~ 0.01* already implies a super-Planckian field range; that is a theoretical discomfort, not a kill-shot. Compatibility is not a detection. String theory sometimes offers inflatons as a catalog. **The catalog cannot currently be tested.**

---

\newpage

## A14. The Primordial Spectrum

**(12)**  *P_R(k) ∝ k^{n_s − 1}*

**(13)**  *n_s ≈ 0.965* (Planck 2018 central value; the error bar, not a second answer, is what later combinations move)

Amplitude *A_s ≈ 2.1 × 10⁻⁹* at the pivot *k = 0.05 Mpc⁻¹*. A scale-invariant Harrison–Zeldovich spectrum is *n_s = 1*. The measured tilt a few percent below 1 is a slow-roll success: *n_s − 1 ≈ −2ε − η* (sign conventions as usual). Planck 2018: *n_s = 0.9649 ± 0.0042* in the baseline combination; later ACT/SPT/Planck combinations move the digit inside 0.96–0.97. Tensor-to-scalar ratio *r = P_T / P_R* is bounded: *r ≲ 0.036* (95%) from BICEP/Keck 2018 plus Planck, with the exact ceiling depending on the likelihood. That kills the 2014 *r ~ 0.2* headline. It does not kill a local burst.

The Mukhanov–Sasaki variable *v = z ℛ* quantizes the curvature twitch *ℛ* on a rigid expanding background. Modes freeze when they exit the Hubble radius (*k = aH*) and re-enter later as density seeds *δρ/ρ ~ 10⁻⁵* — the same order as *ΔT/T* on the leftover glow. Acoustic peaks in the CMB and the baryon acoustic feature in galaxy surveys (A18) are that twitch, oscillated and projected. Non-Gaussianity *f_NL* is consistent with 0 at the level slow-roll predicts (*f_NL ~ n_s − 1*). A large local *f_NL* would have been a different, colder story.

Hot: *A_s*, *n_s < 1*, acoustic physics. Warm: a slow-roll burst as the source. Cold: a named inflaton, a detected tensor background.

---

\newpage

## A15. Eternal Inflation and the Measure Problem

If the quantum kick *δφ ~ H/2π* per Hubble time exceeds the classical roll down the potential, inflation self-reproduces: volume that is still inflating grows faster than volume that has exited. Pocket nucleations in a false-vacuum sea (Coleman–De Luccia) are a related picture with bubbles instead of a slowly rolling field. Both produce a multiverse of the Level II kind (A37). Neither is a detection.

The measure problem is that “most” is not defined. Volume-weighted measures favor pockets that inflate longest. Pocket-weighted measures count nucleations. Observer-weighted measures try to condition on galaxies or on Boltzmann brains. They disagree, sometimes by infinite factors. Anthropic cuts on *Λ* (A41) inherit this fog: Weinberg’s upper bound is a real inequality; turning it into a probability needs a measure you do not have.

Borde–Guth–Vilenkin (BGV): if the average Hubble rate along a past-directed geodesic is positive, that geodesic is past-incomplete. Eternal inflation, on this theorem, is not a past-eternal block. It does not prove a kitchen-time “before.” It proves that the inflating congruence cannot be extended indefinitely into the past as a regular, expanding spacetime. What replaces the incompleteness is A44’s problem, not a filmed first tick.

Temperature: local burst, warm (A13–A14). Eternal froth and a predictive measure, cold-to-warm at best. A landscape of string vacua as the menu, untestable by (18).

---

\newpage

## A16. Sensitivity: From Quantum Noise to BKL Chaos

Inflationary *δφ* is chaotic in the colloquial sense: a *10⁻⁵* twitch, stretched across the sky, becomes the difference between a void and a cluster. It is not always chaotic in the formal attractor sense. Slow-roll backgrounds are often attractors in field space; the *noise* on top of them is what gets amplified into *δρ/ρ*. Do not sell “chaos” as a single noun for both.

BKL (Belinski–Khalatnikov–Lifshitz): generic spacelike singularities in classical GR show Mixmaster behavior — chaotic Bianchi IX oscillations, a sequence of Kasner epochs with exponents that bounce in a deterministic but exponentially sensitive map.
